From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22.0, F22.8, F22.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm here because... honestly, for almost two years now, I’ve been dealing with this situation at work. I’m a composer and music director, and after a big commission a while back, I just haven't been able to shake the certainty that a few people in the ensemble are quietly trying to undermine my work. It’s in the way they handle schedule changes, or their seating choices, and just... little comments during rehearsals. They’ll show me emails or notes that supposedly explain why things are happening the way they are, but I know what's really going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it’s possible that you could be mistaken about what’s happening with the ensemble?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I don't think I'm mistaken. I know they show me those emails and notes that supposedly explain everything away, but... well, like I was just saying, I just can't shake the certainty of it. For almost two years now, ever since that commission, it's just been obvious to me that a few of them are using those schedule changes, their seating choices, and those little comments during rehearsals to quietly undermine my work. Even with their supposed proof, I wouldn't be here if I wasn't absolutely sure of what I'm seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices when no one else was around?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I've never heard voices or anything like that. It's just the things I actually see and hear them doing during rehearsals.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other fixed beliefs like this about people or events outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. It's... it's really just this specific situation at work. Outside of the ensemble, everything in my life is pretty normal. I don't have any issues with my friends or family or anything like that. It's strictly just about my work and what those few people are doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that your thoughts or actions are being controlled or influenced by an outside force?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I don't feel like anyone is controlling my mind or my actions, or putting thoughts in my head. I'm completely in control of myself. It's just... I'm just reacting to what they are doing to me. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that things on the radio, TV, or in the newspapers are sending you special messages about you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I watch TV and listen to the radio just like anyone else, but I don't feel like there are any hidden messages for me. It's just regular news and shows. The only thing I'm dealing with is what's happening right in front of me at work.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone at the ensemble been deliberately recording or sharing your rehearsals in a way that you believe is secretly meant to harm you?\n\nPatient: No, no, I haven't noticed anything like that. No one's recording me or... or sharing tapes of my rehearsals or anything. It's really just the way they act toward me in person, the things they do to sabotage my work directly. There's no secret recordings or anything like that going around.\n\nDoctor: Apart from what’s happening at work, do you believe anyone else is trying to harm, monitor, or interfere with you?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just the people at the ensemble. Outside of work, my life is pretty normal. I don't feel like anyone else is following me or trying to mess with my life. My friends and family are fine. It's just... it's strictly confined to what's happening with the musicians and staff at my job. That's where all the interference is coming from.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you, on a scale from 0 to 100, that the ensemble is deliberately undermining your work?\n\nPatient: I'd say 100. I mean, I am completely certain. That's actually why I'm here, because... well, for almost two years now, I've just been unable to shake the certainty of it. \n\nIt started right after the commission. It's just a few people in the ensemble, really, but they started using schedule changes, weird seating choices, and... and just these little comments in rehearsals to quietly undermine my work. And they'll try to show me emails or notes that supposedly explain it all away, like it's just administrative mistakes or whatever, but I know what's actually happening. So, yeah, 100 percent.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the people at the ensemble are specifically trying to harm you or sabotage you, or is it more that they are spying on you?\n\nPatient: It's definitely sabotage. I don't... I haven't seen any signs that they're spying on me. Like I mentioned, they don't seem to care about what I do outside of the music, so there's no one following me or keeping tabs on my personal life. It's just about ruining my work right there in the room with those schedule changes and comments. So, yeah, they are specifically trying to harm my career, not spy on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your thinking or speech, like becoming hard for other people to follow?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I feel like my thinking is completely clear. I mean, I can communicate just fine—nobody has told me I'm hard to understand or anything. I'm just trying to deal with the situation at work, but my mind itself feels totally normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your work, relationships, or daily functioning since this started?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, outside of this whole issue with the ensemble, everything else is pretty much the same. I still meet my deadlines, and I'm keeping up with my composing just fine. I still go for a run most mornings. And things at home with my wife are basically steady. So, aside from dealing with that specific situation at work, my day-to-day life hasn't really changed.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the ensemble is using some kind of impossible or supernatural means to undermine you?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. I don't think there's anything supernatural or impossible going on. I mean, they aren't using magic or mind control or anything crazy. It's just... it's just regular, everyday sabotage. Like I said before, it's the schedule changes, the little comments, the whispering. They're just using normal, underhanded tactics to mess with my work.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's just the things I actually see and hear them doing during rehearsals.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other fixed beliefs like this about people or events outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. It's... it's really just this specific situation at work. Outside of the ensemble, everything in my life is pretty normal. I don't have any issues with my friends or family or anything like that. It's strictly just about my work and what those few people are doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that your thoughts or actions are being controlled or influenced by an outside force?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I don't feel like anyone is controlling my mind or my actions, or putting thoughts in my head. I'm completely in control of myself. It's just... I'm just reacting to what they are doing to me. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that things on the radio, TV, or in the newspapers are sending you special messages about you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I watch TV and listen to the radio just like anyone else, but I don't feel like there are any hidden messages for me. It's just regular news and shows. The only thing I'm dealing with is what's happening right in front of me at work.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone at the ensemble been deliberately recording or sharing your rehearsals in a way that you believe is secretly meant to harm you?\n\nPatient: No, no, I haven't noticed anything like that. No one's recording me or... or sharing tapes of my rehearsals or anything. It's really just the way they act toward me in person, the things they do to sabotage my work directly. There's no secret recordings or anything like that going around.\n\nDoctor: Apart from what’s happening at work, do you believe anyone else is trying to harm, monitor, or interfere with you?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just the people at the ensemble. Outside of work, my life is pretty normal. I don't feel like anyone else is following me or trying to mess with my life. My friends and family are fine. It's just... it's strictly confined to what's happening with the musicians and staff at my job. That's where all the interference is coming from.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you, on a scale from 0 to 100, that the ensemble is deliberately undermining your work?\n\nPatient: I'd say 100. I mean, I am completely certain. That's actually why I'm here, because... well, for almost two years now, I've just been unable to shake the certainty of it. \n\nIt started right after the commission. It's just a few people in the ensemble, really, but they started using schedule changes, weird seating choices, and... and just these little comments in rehearsals to quietly undermine my work. And they'll try to show me emails or notes that supposedly explain it all away, like it's just administrative mistakes or whatever, but I know what's actually happening. So, yeah, 100 percent.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the people at the ensemble are specifically trying to harm you or sabotage you, or is it more that they are spying on you?\n\nPatient: It's definitely sabotage. I don't... I haven't seen any signs that they're spying on me. Like I mentioned, they don't seem to care about what I do outside of the music, so there's no one following me or keeping tabs on my personal life. It's just about ruining my work right there in the room with those schedule changes and comments. So, yeah, they are specifically trying to harm my career, not spy on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your thinking or speech, like becoming hard for other people to follow?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I feel like my thinking is completely clear. I mean, I can communicate just fine—nobody has told me I'm hard to understand or anything. I'm just trying to deal with the situation at work, but my mind itself feels totally normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your work, relationships, or daily functioning since this started?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, outside of this whole issue with the ensemble, everything else is pretty much the same. I still meet my deadlines, and I'm keeping up with my composing just fine. I still go for a run most mornings. And things at home with my wife are basically steady. So, aside from dealing with that specific situation at work, my day-to-day life hasn't really changed.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the ensemble is using some kind of impossible or supernatural means to undermine you?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. I don't think there's anything supernatural or impossible going on. I mean, they aren't using magic or mind control or anything crazy. It's just... it's just regular, everyday sabotage. Like I said before, it's the schedule changes, the little comments, the whispering. They're just using normal, underhanded tactics to mess with my work.\n\nDoctor: Has this concern caused problems for your work or other parts of your life?\n\nPatient: Not major problems, no. I'd say the main consequence is really just that rehearsals and... well, professional emails take a lot more energy now. I end up rereading decisions a few times, and sometimes I have to ask for extra clarification just to make sure things are clear and I know exactly what they're doing. But I haven't lost any work over it, and I certainly haven't stopped taking care of myself or anything like that. I'm still doing my job, it just takes a bit more effort to manage the communication with them.\n\nDoctor: What is the main evidence that makes you certain the ensemble is undermining you?\n\nPatient: Well, it’s been going on for almost two years now, ever since that commission. The main evidence is really just the pattern of what they do. A few people in the ensemble keep using these sudden schedule changes, or they'll mess with the seating choices. And then there are those little comments they make during rehearsals. \n\nIt’s all set up to quietly undermine my work. I mean, they’ll show me emails or rehearsal notes that supposedly explain why a change was made—like it was just a logistical thing or a simple mistake—but... I just haven't been able to shake the certainty that it's intentional. You can just tell when something isn't an accident, no matter what kind of proof they try to hand you.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. They’ll show me emails or notes that supposedly explain why things are happening the way they are, but I know what's really going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it’s possible that you could be mistaken about what’s happening with the ensemble?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I don't think I'm mistaken. I know they show me those emails and notes that supposedly explain everything away, but... well, like I was just saying, I just can't shake the certainty of it. For almost two years now, ever since that commission, it's just been obvious to me that a few of them are using those schedule changes, their seating choices, and those little comments during rehearsals to quietly undermine my work. Even with their supposed proof, I wouldn't be here if I wasn't absolutely sure of what I'm seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices when no one else was around?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I've never heard voices or anything like that. It's just the things I actually see and hear them doing during rehearsals.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other fixed beliefs like this about people or events outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. It's... it's really just this specific situation at work. Outside of the ensemble, everything in my life is pretty normal. I don't have any issues with my friends or family or anything like that. It's strictly just about my work and what those few people are doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that your thoughts or actions are being controlled or influenced by an outside force?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I don't feel like anyone is controlling my mind or my actions, or putting thoughts in my head. I'm completely in control of myself. It's just... I'm just reacting to what they are doing to me. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that things on the radio, TV, or in the newspapers are sending you special messages about you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I watch TV and listen to the radio just like anyone else, but I don't feel like there are any hidden messages for me. It's just regular news and shows. The only thing I'm dealing with is what's happening right in front of me at work.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone at the ensemble been deliberately recording or sharing your rehearsals in a way that you believe is secretly meant to harm you?\n\nPatient: No, no, I haven't noticed anything like that. No one's recording me or... or sharing tapes of my rehearsals or anything. It's really just the way they act toward me in person, the things they do to sabotage my work directly. There's no secret recordings or anything like that going around.\n\nDoctor: Apart from what’s happening at work, do you believe anyone else is trying to harm, monitor, or interfere with you?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just the people at the ensemble. Outside of work, my life is pretty normal. I don't feel like anyone else is following me or trying to mess with my life. My friends and family are fine. It's just... it's strictly confined to what's happening with the musicians and staff at my job. That's where all the interference is coming from.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you, on a scale from 0 to 100, that the ensemble is deliberately undermining your work?\n\nPatient: I'd say 100. I mean, I am completely certain. That's actually why I'm here, because... well, for almost two years now, I've just been unable to shake the certainty of it. \n\nIt started right after the commission. It's just a few people in the ensemble, really, but they started using schedule changes, weird seating choices, and... and just these little comments in rehearsals to quietly undermine my work. And they'll try to show me emails or notes that supposedly explain it all away, like it's just administrative mistakes or whatever, but I know what's actually happening. So, yeah, 100 percent.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the people at the ensemble are specifically trying to harm you or sabotage you, or is it more that they are spying on you?\n\nPatient: It's definitely sabotage. I don't... I haven't seen any signs that they're spying on me. Like I mentioned, they don't seem to care about what I do outside of the music, so there's no one following me or keeping tabs on my personal life. It's just about ruining my work right there in the room with those schedule changes and comments. So, yeah, they are specifically trying to harm my career, not spy on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your thinking or speech, like becoming hard for other people to follow?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I feel like my thinking is completely clear. I mean, I can communicate just fine—nobody has told me I'm hard to understand or anything. I'm just trying to deal with the situation at work, but my mind itself feels totally normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your work, relationships, or daily functioning since this started?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, outside of this whole issue with the ensemble, everything else is pretty much the same. I still meet my deadlines, and I'm keeping up with my composing just fine. I still go for a run most mornings. And things at home with my wife are basically steady. So, aside from dealing with that specific situation at work, my day-to-day life hasn't really changed.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the ensemble is using some kind of impossible or supernatural means to undermine you?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. I don't think there's anything supernatural or impossible going on. I mean, they aren't using magic or mind control or anything crazy. It's just... it's just regular, everyday sabotage. Like I said before, it's the schedule changes, the little comments, the whispering. They're just using normal, underhanded tactics to mess with my work.\n\nDoctor: Has this concern caused problems for your work or other parts of your life?\n\nPatient: Not major problems, no. I'd say the main consequence is really just that rehearsals and... well, professional emails take a lot more energy now. I end up rereading decisions a few times, and sometimes I have to ask for extra clarification just to make sure things are clear and I know exactly what they're doing. But I haven't lost any work over it, and I certainly haven't stopped taking care of myself or anything like that. I'm still doing my job, it just takes a bit more effort to manage the communication with them.\n\nDoctor: What is the main evidence that makes you certain the ensemble is undermining you?\n\nPatient: Well, it’s been going on for almost two years now, ever since that commission. The main evidence is really just the pattern of what they do. A few people in the ensemble keep using these sudden schedule changes, or they'll mess with the seating choices. And then there are those little comments they make during rehearsals. \n\nIt’s all set up to quietly undermine my work. I mean, they’ll show me emails or rehearsal notes that supposedly explain why a change was made—like it was just a logistical thing or a simple mistake—but... I just haven't been able to shake the certainty that it's intentional. You can just tell when something isn't an accident, no matter what kind of proof they try to hand you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other beliefs, outside of this work situation, that other people would probably find very unusual or hard to believe?\n\nPatient: No... no, nothing like that. It's really just this one specific situation with the ensemble. Outside of work, everything is completely normal. I don't have any, you know, strange theories about the world or anything else. It's strictly just the professional issues I'm dealing with right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe anyone outside the ensemble is also involved in this, or is it only those people at work?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just them. I haven't noticed anyone outside of work being involved at all. It's strictly those specific people in the ensemble. My friends, family, or just people I interact with outside of that space... they have nothing to do with it. It's completely confined to my professional life with that particular group.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your emotional expression, like feeling less able to show emotion or seeming more withdrawn than usual?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I feel stressed and frustrated about what's happening at work, obviously, but I don't feel withdrawn or numb or anything like that. My emotions are pretty much the same as they've always been. I still laugh at things, I still get upset when things go wrong... I haven't noticed any change in how I show my feelings.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the people at the ensemble are mainly trying to persecute or sabotage you, or is the concern more that they are jealous of you or trying to rival your talent?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I mean, I don't know exactly what their motive is. I haven't figured out if they're jealous of my compositions or if they just want to see me fail for some other reason. I just see what they're actually doing—you know, the missing scores, ignoring my direction during rehearsals. So... yeah, I couldn't tell you if it's about rivalry or if it's just pure sabotage. I just know that they're working against me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
